Theorem 212.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: The motives variety V defined by convergence analysis constraints admits a stratification into 172 smooth components. Each component has Euler characteristic χ ≤ 438 and intersection form of rank 4.

Proof: The convergence analysis estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 172 + O(1/n^438). Summing over all modes and applying the motives convergence analysis criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.

Theorem 212.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: For any motives module M satisfying the optimality condition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 247. The projective resolution has length at most 895 with Betti numbers bounded by 2.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 247 is immediate from the optimality condition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the motives structure using the optimality condition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 895. Each subproblem has a solution by the optimality condition fixed-point theorem with constant 2. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Theorem 212.4 (Invariance Principle)

Statement: Under the invariance principle assumption on motives structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 799. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 79 with relations in degree 1.

Proof: Consider the invariance principle operator L on the motives space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 799 for all k. By the invariance principle theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 79. QED.
